As someone who took pride in his precision and mentorship role, he experienced this shift as a profound threat to his identity and future, and over the following year he became increasingly withdrawn at work, missing safety meetings and turning down chances to train others on the new equipment. Around two years and three months ago, coworkers and his supervisor began noticing that he was having more difficulty concentrating on basic tasks and occasionally stepping away from his forklift mid‑shift, while at home his wife saw him abandon woodworking projects and skip church choir rehearsals. The combination of declining performance reviews, a written warning for safety concerns, and his wife’s alarm at how different he seemed from his usual steady, competitive self led them to contact their primary care physician, who, after ruling out obvious medical causes, referred him to psychiatry when it became clear his functioning had markedly deteriorated.\n- Chief Complaint: “I just don’t care about the things I used to love doing anymore, even my projects in the garage and going out on the river.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I keep thinking the company put some kind of tracking or listening device in the new forklifts and even in my work badge, like they’re watching me specifically and waiting for me to screw up so they can get rid of me, no matter how many times my wife says that’s crazy. \n- S020 (Hallucinations): At work and sometimes even at home in the garage, I hear this man’s voice right behind me saying things like, “You’re dangerous, you’re going to kill someone with that machine,” even when there’s nobody there and no radios on. \n- S022 (Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior): My wife and coworkers say I’ll just stop the forklift in the middle of the aisle or stand in the kitchen at home staring at the counter for ten, fifteen minutes, kind of frozen, and I don’t even realize how long I’ve been just standing there until someone snaps me out of it. \n- S023 (Negative_Symptoms): Most days I feel like I’m moving and talking through mud, so I barely say two words at the dinner table, can’t show much on my face even when my grandkids are over, and I just sit in my chair instead of helping or joking around like I used to. \n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): The weirdest thing is I’ll go out to the garage, look at my half-built fishing boat or the guitars I used to fix up, and I just feel nothing—no excitement, no urge to pick up a tool—so I turn off the light and go back inside. \n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I used to pack away big plates after a shift, but now most nights my wife has to remind me to eat because I’m just not hungry and end up pushing the food around, dropping weight without even trying. \n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): I lie in bed staring at the ceiling for hours, then wake up two or three times a night with my mind racing or hearing that voice, and even on mornings I technically “slept,” I feel like I haven’t really rested at all. \n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): Some days at the warehouse or even just walking from the living room to the bathroom, my legs feel like they’re made of concrete and I move so slow that people have to wait on me, and it takes me forever to get a sentence out when someone asks a simple question. \n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I can’t shake this feeling that I ruined everything—that the layoffs, the safety problems at work, even my wife having to pick up extra shifts are somehow my fault—and I catch myself thinking I don’t deserve to be around my family because all I do is drag them down.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_depressive_episode_occur_concurrently: Around the time I stopped caring about my projects and started dragging myself through the day, that same stretch was when the voice really picked up and I became convinced the company was tracking me, so the hopelessness and the strange things in my head have basically been happening side by side.\n- depressive_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness_(Criterion_C): Even before the voice got so loud and the tracking idea took over, I’d already been feeling worn down, joyless, and guilty most days, and that heavy, depressed feeling has never really let up, even during times when the psychotic stuff has eased a bit.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode_(Criterion_B): There was a stretch last year when my wife thought I was doing a little better mood‑wise and I could get through the day without crying or feeling quite so crushed, but I was still sure my badge was bugged and kept hearing that man’s voice telling me I was dangerous even though I didn’t feel as deeply low right then.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, I’ve gone from being one of the go‑to guys at the warehouse to barely scraping by—getting written up for safety issues, turning down extra hours, skipping church and family get‑togethers, and needing my wife to stay on me about basic things like eating and paying the bills on time.\n- duration: Thinking back, this all really started to snowball not long after the big shake‑up at the warehouse, so it’s been going on for a little over two years now.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The belief that they’re tracking me and the man’s voice criticizing me have been a steady problem for well over a year and a half at this point.\n- depressive_episode_duration: I’ve felt this constant lack of interest, guilt, and heaviness most days since shortly after the restructuring began at work, so it’s been a little over two years when my grandkids are over, and I just sit in my chair instead of helping or joking around like I used to. \n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): The weirdest thing is I’ll go out to the garage, look at my half-built fishing boat or the guitars I used to fix up, and I just feel nothing—no excitement, no urge to pick up a tool—so I turn off the light and go back inside. \n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I used to pack away big plates after a shift, but now most nights my wife has to remind me to eat because I’m just not hungry and end up pushing the food around, dropping weight without even trying. man’s voice telling me I was dangerous even though I didn’t feel as deeply low right then.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, I’ve gone from being one of the go‑to guys at the warehouse to He says awful things like, \"You're dangerous, you're going to kill someone with that machine.\" It sounds completely real, like someone is standing right in the room with me.\n\nDoctor: To help me understand your history, have you ever had any other periods in your life—even years ago—where you felt depressed, down, or lost interest in things, even if it wasn't as severe as this?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever feeling this way before the problems at the warehouse started. I’ve always been a really steady, active guy who loved working on projects and staying busy. This is the first time in my life I've ever felt like this.\n\nDoctor: Did you start hearing this voice only after you began feeling depressed and drained, or have you ever heard it during times when your mood was completely fine?\n\nPatient: Mostly, the voice and that heavy, depressed feeling started around the same time and have gone hand-in-hand. But there was a stretch last year where my wife thought my mood was a bit better and I wasn't feeling quite so crushed, yet I still heard that man's voice and was convinced my work badge was bugged. So even when the depression let up a little, those strange things in my head didn't go away.\n\nDoctor: During that period last year when you still heard the voice and believed your badge was bugged, would you say your mood had returned completely to normal, and how long did that last?\n\nPatient: My mood didn't get completely back to normal, but I definitely felt a bit better—I wasn't crying as much or feeling quite as crushed. That stretch lasted for about a few weeks. But even though I felt a little lighter, I still heard that man's voice and was convinced my work badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of two weeks or more where you felt completely back to your normal, non-depressed self, but you were still hearing the voice or having those paranoid thoughts?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I never felt completely back to my normal self during that time, just a little bit lighter. But during those few weeks when I wasn't feeling quite so crushed, I definitely still heard that man's voice calling me dangerous and believed my work badge was bugged.

But even though I felt a little lighter, I still heard that man's voice and was convinced my work badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of two weeks or more where you felt completely back to your normal, non-depressed self, but you were still hearing the voice or having those paranoid thoughts?\n\nPatient: No, I never felt completely back to my normal self during that time, just a little bit lighter. But during those few weeks when I wasn't feeling quite so crushed, I definitely still heard that man's voice calling me dangerous and believed my work badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Before this difficult period started two years ago, did you ever hear voices or feel like people were watching, spying on, or bugging you?"

I never had any of those strange experiences back then.\n\nDoctor: During those few weeks when you felt a bit lighter, did you find that you started to regain any interest in your hobbies, like your woodworking or going to the river, or did you still have no interest in them at all?"

So even when the depression let up a little, those strange things in my head didn't go away.\n\nDoctor: During that period last year when you still heard the voice and believed your badge was bugged, would you say your mood had returned completely to normal, and how long did that last?\n\nPatient: My mood didn't get completely back to normal, but I definitely felt a bit better—I wasn't crying as much or feeling quite as crushed. That stretch lasted for about a few weeks. But even though I felt a little lighter, I still heard that man's voice and was convinced my work badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of two weeks or more where you felt completely back to your normal, non-depressed self, but you were still hearing the voice or having those paranoid thoughts?\n\nPatient: No, I never felt completely back to my normal self during that time, just a little bit lighter. But during those few weeks when I wasn't feeling quite so crushed, I definitely still heard that man's voice calling me dangerous and believed my work badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Before this difficult period started two years ago, did you ever hear voices or feel like people were watching, spying on, or bugging you?\n\nPatient: No, I never experienced anything like hearing voices or feeling watched before all this started. Before the warehouse restructuring, I was just my usual steady self, focusing on my woodworking, my family, and doing my job. I never had any of those strange experiences back then.\n\nDoctor: During those few weeks when you felt a bit lighter, did you find that you started to regain any interest in your hobbies, like your woodworking or going to the river, or did you still have no interest in them at all?\n\nPatient: No, I still didn't have any interest in them at all. Even though I felt a little lighter, I would go out to the garage, look at my half-built boat, and just feel completely blank. I couldn't bring myself to pick up a tool, so I'd just turn off the light and go back inside.\n\nDoctor: Aside from that brief period last year, has there ever been any other time over the past two years where you felt completely free of depression, yet you still heard the voice or felt that your badge was bugged?"

No, there hasn't been any other time besides that stretch last year. Usually, the heavy depression and the voices go hand-in-hand, and they've just been a constant weight together. That one period was the only exception where my mood lifted a little bit while the other things stayed.

It's like all the joy has just been drained out of me, and I can't bring myself to do any of it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and have you ever experienced similar periods of low mood in the past?\n\nPatient: I've been feeling this way for a little over two years now, starting not long after the big restructuring at my warehouse. I've never experienced a low period like this in the past; it all really began with that stress at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods of time where you felt the opposite of depressed—unusually energetic, overly happy, needing much less sleep than usual, or feeling like your mind was racing?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt unusually energetic or happy like that; it's actually been the exact opposite, where I just feel heavy, slow, and completely drained. My mind does race sometimes when I'm lying in bed at night, but that's just from worrying and hearing that voice, not because I have any energy.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned hearing a voice when you're trying to sleep. Does it sound like a real person speaking to you from outside your head, and what does it say?\n\nPatient: Yes, it sounds like a real man's voice speaking from right behind me, even when I know there's absolutely nobody there. He says awful things like, \"You're dangerous, you're going to kill someone with that machine.\" It sounds completely real, like someone is standing right in the room with me.\n\nDoctor: To help me understand your history, have you ever had any other periods in your life—even years ago—where you felt depressed, down, or lost interest in things, even if it wasn't as severe as this?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever feeling this way before the problems at the warehouse started. I’ve always been a really steady, active guy who loved working on projects and staying busy. This is the first time in my life I've ever felt like this.\n\nDoctor: Did you start hearing this voice only after you began feeling depressed and drained, or have you ever heard it during times when your mood was completely fine?\n\nPatient: Mostly, the voice and that heavy, depressed feeling started around the same time and have gone hand-in-hand. But there was a stretch last year where my wife thought my mood was a bit better and I wasn't feeling quite so crushed, yet I still heard that man's voice and was convinced my work badge was bugged. So even when the depression let up a little, those strange things in my head didn't go away.\n\nDoctor: During that period last year when you still heard the voice and believed your badge was bugged, would you say your mood had returned completely to normal, and how long did that last?\n\nPatient: My mood didn't get completely back to normal, but I definitely felt a bit better—I wasn't crying as much or feeling quite as crushed. That stretch lasted for about a few weeks. But even though I felt a little lighter, I still heard that man's voice and was convinced my work badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of two weeks or more where you felt completely back to your normal, non-depressed self, but you were still hearing the voice or having those paranoid thoughts?\n\nPatient: No, I never felt completely back to my normal self during that time, just a little bit lighter. But during those few weeks when I wasn't feeling quite so crushed, I definitely still heard that man's voice calling me dangerous and believed my work badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Before this difficult period started two years ago, did you ever hear voices or feel like people were watching, spying on, or bugging you?\n\nPatient: No, I never experienced anything like hearing voices or feeling watched before all this started. Before the warehouse restructuring, I was just my usual steady self, focusing on my woodworking, my family, and doing my job. I never had any of those strange experiences back then.\n\nDoctor: During those few weeks when you felt a bit lighter, did you find that you started to regain any interest in your hobbies, like your woodworking or going to the river, or did you still have no interest in them at all?\n\nPatient: No, I still didn't have any interest in them at all. Even though I felt a little lighter, I would go out to the garage, look at my half-built boat, and just feel completely blank. I couldn't bring myself to pick up a tool, so I'd just turn off the light and go back inside.\n\nDoctor: Aside from that brief period last year, has there ever been any other time over the past two years where you felt completely free of depression, yet you still heard the voice or felt that your badge was bugged?\n\nPatient: No, there hasn't been any other time besides that stretch last year. Usually, the heavy depression and the voices go hand-in-hand, and they've just been a constant weight together. That one period was the only exception where my mood lifted a little bit while the other things stayed.\n\nDoctor: When you believed your work badge was bugged, what did you think was the reason they were tracking or spying on you?"

Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.
